          <p>(2) all debts, obligations, or other liabilities of the converting organization continue as debts, obligations, or other liabilities of the converted organization;</p>
          <p>(3) an action or proceeding pending by or against the converting organization may be continued as if the conversion had not occurred or as actions or proceedings by or against the converted organization;</p>
          <p>(4) except as prohibited by law other than this chapter, all of the rights, privileges, immunities, powers, and purposes of the converting organization remain vested in the converted organization; and</p>
          <p>(5) the conversion does not dissolve a converting corporation for the purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.701">302A.701</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.791">302A.791</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.691.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Foreign organization.</span></h2>
          <p>A converted organization that is a foreign organization consents to the jurisdiction of the courts of this state to enforce any debt, obligation, or other liability for which the converting corporation is liable if, before the conversion, the converting corporation was subject to suit in this state on the debt, obligation, or other liability. A converted organization that is a foreign organization and not authorized to transact business in this state appoints the secretary of state as its agent for service of process for purposes of enforcing a debt, obligation, or other liability under this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.302A.691.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/199/#laws.14.28.0">2004 c 199 art 14 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/157/#laws.2.16.0">2014 c 157 art 2 s 16</a>,31; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/170/#laws.0.25.0">2014 c 170 s 25</a>,26; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/39/#laws.0.12.0">2015 c 39 s 12</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.302A.692">
        <h1 class="shn">302A.692 RESTRICTIONS ON APPROVAL OF CONVERSIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.692.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Personal liability of shareholder.</span></h2>
          <p>If a shareholder of a converting corporation will have personal liability with respect to a converted organization, approval or amendment of a plan of conversion is ineffective without the consent of the shareholder, unless:</p>
          <p>(1) a valid shareholder control agreement of the converting corporation provides for approval of a conversion and imposition of personal liability without the consent of all shareholders against whom personal liability is imposed;</p>
          <p>(2) the shareholder has consented in writing to the provision of the valid shareholder control agreement; and</p>
          <p>(3) the shareholder does not exercise dissenter's rights pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.471">302A.471</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.692.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Consent.</span></h2>
          <p>A shareholder does not give the consent required by subdivision 1 merely by consenting to a provision of a shareholder control agreement or any other agreement that permits the shareholder control agreement or any other agreement to be amended with the consent of fewer than all shareholders or parties to the other agreement.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.302A.692.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/157/#laws.2.17.0">2014 c 157 art 2 s 17</a>,31; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/39/#laws.0.13.0">2015 c 39 s 13</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">DISSOLUTION</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.302A.701">
        <h1 class="shn">302A.701 METHODS OF DISSOLUTION.</h1>
        <p>A corporation may be dissolved:</p>
        <p>(a) before the issuance of shares, pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.711">302A.711</a>;</p>
        <p>(b) after the issuance of shares, pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.721">302A.721 </a>to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.7291">302A.7291</a>;</p>
        <p>(c) by order of a court pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.741">302A.741</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.765">302A.765</a>; or</p>
        <p>(d) by the secretary of state according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.821">302A.821</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.302A.701.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/270/#laws.0.98.0">1981 c 270 s 98</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/49/#laws.0.21.0">1991 c 49 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/128/#laws.3.3.0">1995 c 128 art 3 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/233/#laws.1.15.0">2008 c 233 art 1 s 15</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.302A.711">
        <h1 class="shn">302A.711 VOLUNTARY DISSOLUTION BEFORE ISSUANCE OF SHARES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.711.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Manner.</span></h2>
          <p>A corporation that has not issued shares may be dissolved by the incorporators or directors in the manner set forth in this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.711.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Articles of dissolution.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A majority of the incorporators or directors shall sign articles of dissolution containing:</p>
          <p>(1) the name of the corporation;</p>
          <p>(2) the date of incorporation;</p>
          <p>(3) a statement that shares have not been issued;</p>
          <p>(4) a statement that all consideration received from subscribers for shares to be issued, less expenses incurred in the organization of the corporation, has been returned to the subscribers; and</p>
          <p>(5) a statement that no debts remain unpaid.</p>
          <p>(b) The articles of dissolution shall be filed with the secretary of state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.711.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Effective date.</span></h2>
          <p>When the articles of dissolution have been filed with the secretary of state, the corporation is dissolved.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.711.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Certificate.</span></h2>
          <p>The secretary of state shall issue to the dissolved corporation or its legal representative a certificate of dissolution that contains:</p>
          <p>(a) the name of the corporation;</p>
          <p>(b) the date the articles of dissolution were filed with the secretary of state; and</p>
          <p>(c) a statement that the corporation is dissolved.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.302A.711.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/270/#laws.0.99.0">1981 c 270 s 99</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/17/#laws.0.55.0">1993 c 17 s 55</a>,56; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/106/#laws.0.5.0">2011 c 106 s 5</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.302A.721">
        <h1 class="shn">302A.721 VOLUNTARY DISSOLUTION AFTER ISSUANCE OF SHARES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.721.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Manner.</span></h2>
          <p>After the issuance of shares, a corporation may be dissolved when authorized in the manner set forth in this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.721.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Notice; approval.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If the corporation has outstanding shares:</p>
          <p>(1) Written notice shall be given to each shareholder, whether or not entitled to vote at a meeting of shareholders, within the time and in the manner provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.435">302A.435</a> for notice of meetings of shareholders and, whether the meeting is a regular or a special meeting, shall state that a purpose of the meeting is to consider dissolving the corporation.</p>
          <p>(2) The proposed dissolution shall be submitted for approval at a meeting of shareholders. If the proposed dissolution is approved at a meeting by the affirmative vote of the holders of a majority of the voting power of all shares entitled to vote, the dissolution shall be commenced.</p>
          <p>(b) If the corporation no longer has any outstanding shares, then the directors may authorize and commence the dissolution. If the directors take that action, then the notice of dissolution filed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.723">302A.723</a> shall so reflect and the directors shall have the right to revoke the dissolution proceedings in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.731">302A.731</a>, subdivision 1.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.302A.721.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/270/#laws.0.100.0">1981 c 270 s 100</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/497/#laws.0.58.0">1982 c 497 s 58</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/233/#laws.1.16.0">2008 c 233 art 1 s 16</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.302A.723">
        <h1 class="shn">302A.723 FILING NOTICE OF INTENT TO DISSOLVE; EFFECT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.723.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Contents.</span></h2>
          <p>If dissolution of the corporation is approved pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.721#stat.302A.721.2">302A.721, subdivision 2</a>, the corporation shall file with the secretary of state a notice of intent to dissolve. The notice shall contain:</p>
          <p>(a) the name of the corporation;</p>
          <p>(b) the date and place of the meeting at which the resolution was approved pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.721#stat.302A.721.2">302A.721, subdivision 2</a>; and</p>
          <p>(c) a statement that the requisite vote of the shareholders was received, or that the requisite shareholders entitled to vote signed a written action.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.723.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Winding up.</span></h2>
          <p>When the notice of intent to dissolve has been filed with the secretary of state, and subject to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.731">302A.731</a>, the corporation shall cease to carry on its business, except to the extent necessary for the winding up of the corporation. The shareholders shall retain the right to revoke the dissolution proceedings in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.731">302A.731</a> and the right to remove directors or fill vacancies on the board. The corporate existence continues to the extent necessary to wind up the affairs of the corporation until the dissolution proceedings are revoked or articles of dissolution are filed with the secretary of state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.723.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Remedies continued.</span></h2>
          <p>The filing with the secretary of state of a notice of intent to dissolve does not affect any remedy in favor of the corporation or any remedy against it or its directors, officers, or shareholders in those capacities, except as provided in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.727">302A.727</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.7291">302A.7291</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.781">302A.781</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.302A.723.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/270/#laws.0.101.0">1981 c 270 s 101</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/497/#laws.0.59.0">1982 c 497 s 59</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/49/#laws.0.22.0">1991 c 49 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/199/#laws.14.29.0">2004 c 199 art 14 s 29</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.302A.725">
        <h1 class="shn">302A.725 PROCEDURE IN DISSOLUTION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.725.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Collection; payment.</span></h2>
          <p>When a notice of intent to dissolve has been filed with the secretary of state, the board, or the officers acting under the direction of the board, shall proceed as soon as possible:</p>
          <p>(a) To collect or make provision for the collection of all known debts due or owing to the corporation, including unpaid subscriptions for shares;</p>
          <p>(b) Except as provided in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.727">302A.727</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.7291">302A.7291</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.781">302A.781</a>, to pay or make provision for the payment of all known debts, obligations, and liabilities of the corporation according to their priorities; and</p>
          <p>(c) To give notice to creditors and claimants under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.727">302A.727</a> or to proceed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.7291">302A.7291</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.725.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Transfer of assets.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding the provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.661">302A.661</a>, when a notice of intent to dissolve has been filed with the secretary of state, the directors may sell, lease, transfer, or otherwise dispose of all or substantially all of the property and assets of a dissolving corporation without a vote of the shareholders.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.725.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Distribution to shareholders.</span></h2>
          <p>All tangible or intangible property, including money, remaining after the discharge of, or after making adequate provision for the discharge of, the debts, obligations, and liabilities of the corporation shall be distributed to the shareholders in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.551#stat.302A.551.4">302A.551, subdivision 4</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.302A.725.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/270/#laws.0.102.0">1981 c 270 s 102</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/49/#laws.0.23.0">1991 c 49 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/417/#laws.0.8.0">1994 c 417 s 8</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.302A.727">
        <h1 class="shn">302A.727 DISSOLUTION PROCEDURE FOR CORPORATIONS THAT GIVE NOTICE TO CREDITORS AND CLAIMANTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.727.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">When permitted; how given.</span></h2>
          <p>When a notice of intent to dissolve has been filed with the secretary of state, the corporation may give notice of the filing to each creditor of and claimant against the corporation known or unknown, present or future, and contingent or noncontingent. If notice to creditors and claimants is given, it must be given by publishing the notice once each week for four successive weeks in a legal newspaper in the county or counties where the registered office and the principal executive office of the corporation are located and by giving written notice to known creditors and claimants pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.011#stat.302A.011.17">302A.011, subdivision 17</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.727.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Contents.</span></h2>
          <p>The notice to creditors and claimants shall contain:</p>
          <p>(a) a statement that the corporation is in the process of dissolving;</p>
          <p>(b) a statement that the corporation has filed with the secretary of state a notice of intent to dissolve;</p>
          <p>(c) the date of filing the notice of intent to dissolve;</p>
          <p>(d) the address of the office to which written claims against the corporation must be presented; and</p>
          <p>(e) the date by which all the claims must be received, which shall be the later of 90 days after published notice or, with respect to a particular known creditor or claimant, 90 days after the date on which written notice was given to that creditor or claimant. Published notice is deemed given on the date of first publication for the purpose of determining this date.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.727.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Claims against corporations that give notice.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A corporation that gives notice to creditors and claimants has 30 days from the receipt of each claim filed according to the procedures set forth by the corporation on or before the date set forth in the notice to accept or reject the claim by giving written notice to the person submitting it; a claim not expressly rejected in this manner is deemed accepted.</p>
          <p>(b) A creditor or claimant to whom notice is given and whose claim is rejected by the corporation has 60 days from the date of rejection, 180 days from the date the corporation filed with the secretary of state the notice of intent to dissolve, or 90 days after the date on which notice was given to the creditor or claimant, whichever is longer, to pursue any other remedies with respect to the claim.</p>
          <p>(c) A creditor or claimant to whom notice is given who fails to file a claim according to the procedures set forth by the corporation on or before the date set forth in the notice is barred from suing on that claim or otherwise realizing upon or enforcing it, except as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.781">302A.781</a>.</p>
          <p>(d) A creditor or claimant whose claim is rejected by the corporation under paragraph (b) is barred from suing on that claim or otherwise realizing upon or enforcing it, if the creditor or claimant does not initiate legal, administrative, or arbitration proceedings with respect to the claim within the time provided in paragraph (b).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.727.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Articles of dissolution; when filed.</span></h2>
          <p>Articles of dissolution for a corporation that has given notice to creditors and claimants under this section must be filed with the secretary of state after:</p>
          <p>(1) the 90-day period in subdivision 2, paragraph (e), has expired and the payment of claims of all creditors and claimants filing a claim within that period has been made or provided for; or</p>
          <p>(2) the longest of the periods described in subdivision 3, paragraph (b), has expired and there are no pending legal, administrative, or arbitration proceedings by or against the corporation commenced within the time provided in subdivision 3, paragraph (b).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.727.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Contents of articles.</span></h2>
          <p>The articles of dissolution must state:</p>
          <p>(1) the last date on which the notice was given and: (i) that the payment of all creditors and claimants filing a claim within the 90-day period in subdivision 2, paragraph (e), has been made or provided for; or (ii) the date on which the longest of the periods described in subdivision 3, paragraph (b), expired;</p>
          <p>(2) that the remaining property, assets, and claims of the corporation have been distributed among its shareholders in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.551#stat.302A.551.4">302A.551, subdivision 4</a>, or that adequate provision has been made for that distribution; and</p>
          <p>(3) that there are no pending legal, administrative, or arbitration proceedings by or against the corporation commenced within the time provided in subdivision 3, paragraph (b), or that adequate provision has been made for the satisfaction of any judgment, order, or decree that may be entered against it in a pending proceeding.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.302A.727.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/270/#laws.0.103.0">1981 c 270 s 103</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/497/#laws.0.60.0">1982 c 497 s 60</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/543/#laws.0.12.0">1984 c 543 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/104/#laws.0.40.0">1987 c 104 s 40</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/384/#laws.3.13.0">1987 c 384 art 3 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/49/#laws.0.24.0">1991 c 49 s 24</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.302A.729"><b>302A.729</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/49/#laws.0.28.0">1991 c 49 s 28</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.302A.7291">
        <h1 class="shn">302A.7291 DISSOLUTION PROCEDURE FOR CORPORATIONS THAT DO NOT GIVE NOTICE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.7291.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Articles of dissolution; when filed.</span></h2>
          <p>Articles of dissolution for a corporation that has not given notice to creditors and claimants in the manner provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.727">302A.727</a> must be filed with the secretary of state after:</p>
          <p>(1) the payment of claims of all known creditors and claimants has been made or provided for; or</p>
          <p>(2) at least two years have elapsed from the date of filing the notice of intent to dissolve.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.7291.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Contents of articles.</span></h2>
          <p>The articles of dissolution must state:</p>
          <p>(1) if articles of dissolution are being filed pursuant to subdivision 1, clause (1), that all known debts, obligations, and liabilities of the corporation have been paid and discharged or that adequate provision has been made for payment or discharge;</p>
          <p>(2) that the remaining property, assets, and claims of the corporation have been distributed among its shareholders in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.551#stat.302A.551.4">302A.551, subdivision 4</a>, or that adequate provision has been made for that distribution; and</p>
          <p>(3) that there are no pending legal, administrative, or arbitration proceedings by or against the corporation, or that adequate provision has been made for the satisfaction of any judgment, order, or decree that may be entered against it in a pending proceeding.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.7291.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Claims against corporations that do not give notice.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If the corporation has paid or provided for all known creditors or claimants at the time articles of dissolution are filed, a creditor or claimant who does not file a claim or pursue a remedy in a legal, administrative, or arbitration proceeding within two years after the date of filing the notice of intent to dissolve is barred from suing on that claim or otherwise realizing upon or enforcing it.</p>
          <p>(b) If the corporation has not paid or provided for all known creditors and claimants at the time articles of dissolution are filed, a person who does not file a claim or pursue a remedy in a legal, administrative, or arbitration proceeding within two years after the date of filing the notice of intent to dissolve is barred from suing on that claim or otherwise realizing upon or enforcing it, except as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.781">302A.781</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.302A.7291.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/49/#laws.0.25.0">1991 c 49 s 25</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.302A.730"><b>302A.730</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/49/#laws.0.28.0">1991 c 49 s 28</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.302A.731">
        <h1 class="shn">302A.731 REVOCATION OF DISSOLUTION PROCEEDINGS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.731.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Generally.</span></h2>
          <p>Dissolution proceedings commenced pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.721">302A.721</a> may be revoked prior to filing of articles of dissolution.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.731.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Notice to shareholders; approval.</span></h2>
          <p>Written notice shall be given to every shareholder entitled to vote at a shareholders' meeting within the time and in the manner provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.435">302A.435</a> for notice of meetings of shareholders and shall state that a purpose of the meeting is to consider the advisability of revoking the dissolution proceedings. The proposed revocation shall be submitted to the shareholders at the meeting. If the proposed revocation is approved at a meeting by the affirmative vote of the holders of a majority of the voting power of all shares entitled to vote, the dissolution proceedings are revoked.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.731.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Effective date; effect.</span></h2>
          <p>Revocation of dissolution proceedings is effective when a notice of revocation is filed with the secretary of state. The corporation may thereafter resume business.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.302A.731.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/270/#laws.0.105.0">1981 c 270 s 105</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/497/#laws.0.62.0">1982 c 497 s 62</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.302A.733"><b>302A.733</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/49/#laws.0.28.0">1991 c 49 s 28</a>]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.302A.734">
        <h1 class="shn">302A.734 EFFECTIVE DATE OF DISSOLUTION; CERTIFICATE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.734.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Effective date.</span></h2>
          <p>When the articles of dissolution have been filed with the secretary of state, or on a later date or a later time each within 30 days after filing if the articles of dissolution so provide, the corporation is dissolved.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.734.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Certificate.</span></h2>
          <p>The secretary of state shall issue to the corporation or its legal representative a certificate of dissolution that contains:</p>
          <p>(1) the name of the corporation;</p>
          <p>(2) the date the articles of dissolution were filed with the secretary of state or any later effective date or later effective time stated in the articles of dissolution in accordance with subdivision 1; and</p>
          <p>(3) a statement that the corporation is dissolved.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.302A.734.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/49/#laws.0.26.0">1991 c 49 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/311/#laws.1.27.0">2002 c 311 art 1 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/106/#laws.0.6.0">2011 c 106 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/170/#laws.0.27.0">2014 c 170 s 27</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.302A.741">
        <h1 class="shn">302A.741 SUPERVISED VOLUNTARY DISSOLUTION.</h1>
        <p>After the notice of intent to dissolve has been filed with the secretary of state and before a certificate of dissolution has been issued, the corporation or, for good cause shown, a shareholder or creditor may apply to a court within the county in which the registered office of the corporation is situated to have the dissolution conducted or continued under the supervision of the court as provided in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.751">302A.751</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.781">302A.781</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.302A.741.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/270/#laws.0.107.0">1981 c 270 s 107</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/497/#laws.0.64.0">1982 c 497 s 64</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.302A.751">
        <h1 class="shn">302A.751 JUDICIAL INTERVENTION; EQUITABLE REMEDIES OR DISSOLUTION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.751.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">When permitted.</span></h2>
          <p>A court may grant any equitable relief it deems just and reasonable in the circumstances or may dissolve a corporation and liquidate its assets and business:</p>
          <p>(a) In a supervised voluntary dissolution pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.741">302A.741</a>;</p>
          <p>(b) In an action by a shareholder when it is established that:</p>
          <p>(1) the directors or the persons having the authority otherwise vested in the board are deadlocked in the management of the corporate affairs and the shareholders are unable to break the deadlock;</p>
          <p>(2) the directors or those in control of the corporation have acted fraudulently or illegally toward one or more shareholders in their capacities as shareholders or directors, or as officers or employees of a closely held corporation;</p>
          <p>(3) the directors or those in control of the corporation have acted in a manner unfairly prejudicial toward one or more shareholders in their capacities as shareholders or directors of a corporation that is not a publicly held corporation, or as officers or employees of a closely held corporation;</p>
          <p>(4) the shareholders of the corporation are so divided in voting power that, for a period that includes the time when two consecutive regular meetings were held, they have failed to elect successors to directors whose terms have expired or would have expired upon the election and qualification of their successors;</p>
          <p>(5) the corporate assets are being misapplied or wasted; or</p>
          <p>(6) the period of duration as provided in the articles has expired;</p>
          <p>(c) In an action by a creditor when:</p>
          <p>(1) the claim of the creditor has been reduced to judgment and an execution thereon has been returned unsatisfied; or</p>
          <p>(2) the corporation has admitted in writing that the claim of the creditor is due and owing and it is established that the corporation is unable to pay its debts in the ordinary course of business; or</p>
          <p>(d) In an action by the attorney general to dissolve the corporation in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.757">302A.757</a> when it is established that a decree of dissolution is appropriate.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.751.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Buy-out on motion.</span></h2>
          <p>In an action under subdivision 1, clause (b), involving a corporation that is not a publicly held corporation at the time the action is commenced and in which one or more of the circumstances described in that clause is established, the court may, upon motion of a corporation or a shareholder or beneficial owner of shares of the corporation, order the sale by a plaintiff or a defendant of all shares of the corporation held by the plaintiff or defendant to either the corporation or the moving shareholders, whichever is specified in the motion, if the court determines in its discretion that an order would be fair and equitable to all parties under all of the circumstances of the case.</p>
          <p>The purchase price of any shares so sold shall be the fair value of the shares as of the date of the commencement of the action or as of another date found equitable by the court, provided that, if the shares in question are then subject to sale and purchase pursuant to the bylaws of the corporation, a shareholder control agreement, the terms of the shares, or otherwise, the court shall order the sale for the price and on the terms set forth in them, unless the court determines that the price or terms are unreasonable under all the circumstances of the case.</p>
          <p>Within five days after the entry of the order, the corporation shall provide each selling shareholder or beneficial owner with the information it is required to provide under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.473#stat.302A.473.5">302A.473, subdivision 5</a>, paragraph (a).</p>
          <p>If the parties are unable to agree on fair value within 40 days of entry of the order, the court shall determine the fair value of the shares under the provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.473#stat.302A.473.7">302A.473, subdivision 7</a>, and may allow interest or costs as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.473">302A.473</a>, subdivisions 1 and 8.</p>
          <p>The purchase price shall be paid in one or more installments as agreed on by the parties, or, if no agreement can be reached within 40 days of entry of the order, as ordered by the court. Upon entry of an order for the sale of shares under this subdivision and provided that the corporation or the moving shareholders post a bond in adequate amount with sufficient sureties or otherwise satisfy the court that the full purchase price of the shares, plus such additional costs, expenses, and fees as may be awarded, will be paid when due and payable, the selling shareholders shall no longer have any rights or status as shareholders, officers, or directors, except the right to receive the fair value of their shares plus such other amounts as might be awarded.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.751.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Condition of corporation.</span></h2>
          <p>In determining whether to order equitable relief, dissolution, or a buy-out, the court shall take into consideration the financial condition of the corporation but shall not refuse to order equitable relief, dissolution, or a buy-out solely on the ground that the corporation has accumulated or current operating profits.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.751.3a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3a.<span class="headnote">Considerations in granting relief involving closely held corporations.</span></h2>
          <p>In determining whether to order equitable relief, dissolution, or a buy-out, the court shall take into consideration the duty which all shareholders in a closely held corporation owe one another to act in an honest, fair, and reasonable manner in the operation of the corporation and the reasonable expectations of all shareholders as they exist at the inception and develop during the course of the shareholders' relationship with the corporation and with each other. For purposes of this section, any written agreements, including employment agreements and buy-sell agreements, between or among shareholders or between or among one or more shareholders and the corporation are presumed to reflect the parties' reasonable expectations concerning matters dealt with in the agreements.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.751.3b">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3b.<span class="headnote">Dissolution as remedy.</span></h2>
          <p>In deciding whether to order dissolution, the court shall consider whether lesser relief suggested by one or more parties, such as any form of equitable relief, a buy-out, or a partial liquidation, would be adequate to permanently relieve the circumstances established under subdivision 1, clause (b) or (c). Lesser relief may be ordered in any case where it would be appropriate under all the facts and circumstances of the case.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.751.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Expenses.</span></h2>
          <p>If the court finds that a party to a proceeding brought under this section has acted arbitrarily, vexatiously, or otherwise not in good faith, it may in its discretion award reasonable expenses, including attorneys' fees and disbursements, to any of the other parties.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.751.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Venue; parties.</span></h2>
          <p>Proceedings under this section shall be brought in a court within the county in which the registered office of the corporation is located. It is not necessary to make shareholders parties to the action or proceeding unless relief is sought against them personally.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.302A.751.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/270/#laws.0.108.0">1981 c 270 s 108</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/497/#laws.0.65.0">1982 c 497 s 65</a>,66; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/368/#laws.0.9.0">1983 c 368 s 9</a>-11; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/431/#laws.0.3.0">1986 c 431 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/417/#laws.0.9.0">1994 c 417 s 9</a>-11; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/106/#laws.0.7.0">2011 c 106 s 7</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.302A.753">
        <h1 class="shn">302A.753 PROCEDURE IN INVOLUNTARY OR SUPERVISED VOLUNTARY DISSOLUTION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.753.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Action before hearing.</span></h2>
          <p>In dissolution proceedings the court may issue injunctions, appoint receivers with all powers and duties the court directs, take other actions required to preserve the corporate assets wherever situated, and carry on the business of the corporation until a full hearing can be held.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.753.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Action after hearing.</span></h2>
          <p>After a full hearing has been held, upon whatever notice the court directs to be given to all parties to the proceedings and to any other parties in interest designated by the court, the court may appoint a receiver to collect the corporate assets, including all amounts owing to the corporation by subscribers on account of any unpaid portion of the consideration for the issuance of shares. In addition to the powers set forth in chapter 576, a receiver has authority, subject to the order of the court, to continue the business of the corporation and to sell, lease, transfer, or otherwise dispose of all or any of the property and assets of the corporation either at public or private sale.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.753.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Discharge of obligations.</span></h2>
          <p>The assets of the corporation or the proceeds resulting from a sale, lease, transfer, or other disposition shall be applied in the order of priority set forth in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/576.51">576.51</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.753.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Remainder to shareholders.</span></h2>
          <p>After payment of the expenses of receivership and claims of creditors duly proved, the remaining assets, if any, shall be distributed to the shareholders in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.551#stat.302A.551.4">302A.551, subdivision 4</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.302A.753.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/270/#laws.0.109.0">1981 c 270 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/143/#laws.3.1.0">2012 c 143 art 3 s 1</a>,2</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.302A.755">
        <h1 class="shn">302A.755 QUALIFICATIONS OF RECEIVERS; POWERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.755.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Qualifications.</span></h2>
          <p>Any person qualified under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/576.26">576.26</a> may be appointed as receiver. A receiver shall give bond as required by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/576.27">576.27</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.755.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Powers.</span></h2>
          <p>A receiver may sue and defend all actions as receiver of the corporation. The court appointing the receiver has exclusive jurisdiction over the corporation, the receiver, and all receivership property pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/576.23">576.23</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.302A.755.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/270/#laws.0.110.0">1981 c 270 s 110</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/143/#laws.3.3.0">2012 c 143 art 3 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.302A.757">
        <h1 class="shn">302A.757 ACTION BY ATTORNEY GENERAL.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.757.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">When permitted.</span></h2>
          <p>A corporation may be dissolved involuntarily by a decree of a court in this state in an action filed by the attorney general when it is established that:</p>
          <p>(a) the articles and certificate of incorporation were procured through fraud;</p>
          <p>(b) the corporation was incorporated for a purpose not permitted by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.101">302A.101</a>;</p>
          <p>(c) the corporation failed to comply with the requirements of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.021">302A.021</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.155">302A.155</a> essential to incorporation under or election to become governed by this chapter;</p>
          <p>(d) the corporation has flagrantly violated a provision of this chapter, or has violated a provision of this chapter more than once, or has violated more than one provision of this chapter; or</p>
          <p>(e) the corporation has acted, or failed to act, in a manner that constitutes surrender or abandonment of the corporate franchise, privileges, or enterprise.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.757.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Notice to corporation; correction.</span></h2>
          <p>An action shall not be commenced under this section until 30 days after notice to the corporation by the attorney general of the reason for the filing of the action. If the reason for filing the action is an act that the corporation has done, or omitted to do, and the act or omission may be corrected by an amendment of the articles or bylaws or by performance of or abstention from the act, the attorney general shall give the corporation 30 additional days in which to effect the correction before filing the action.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.302A.757.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/270/#laws.0.111.0">1981 c 270 s 111</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.302A.759">
        <h1 class="shn">302A.759 FILING CLAIMS IN PROCEEDINGS TO DISSOLVE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.759.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Manner and form.</span></h2>
          <p>In proceedings referred to in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.751">302A.751</a> to dissolve a corporation, the court may require all creditors and claimants of the corporation to file their claims pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/576.49">576.49</a>. The receiver or any party in interest may object to any claim pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/576.50">576.50</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.759.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/143/#laws.3.39.0">2012 c 143 art 3 s 39</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.302A.759.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/270/#laws.0.112.0">1981 c 270 s 112</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/1/3/#laws.1.82.0">1Sp1986 c 3 art 1 s 82</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/143/#laws.3.4.0">2012 c 143 art 3 s 4</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.302A.761">
        <h1 class="shn">302A.761 DISCONTINUANCE OF DISSOLUTION PROCEEDINGS.</h1>
        <p>The involuntary or supervised voluntary dissolution of a corporation shall be discontinued at any time during the dissolution proceedings when it is established that cause for dissolution no longer exists. When this is established, the court shall dismiss the proceedings and direct the receiver, if any, to redeliver to the corporation all its remaining property and assets and to file a final report pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/576.38#stat.576.38.3">576.38, subdivision 3</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.302A.761.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/270/#laws.0.113.0">1981 c 270 s 113</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/143/#laws.3.5.0">2012 c 143 art 3 s 5</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.302A.763">
        <h1 class="shn">302A.763 DECREE OF DISSOLUTION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.763.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">When entered.</span></h2>
          <p>In an involuntary or supervised voluntary dissolution after the costs and expenses of the proceedings and all debts, obligations, and liabilities of the corporation have been paid or discharged and all of its remaining property and assets have been distributed to its shareholders or, if its property and assets are not sufficient to satisfy and discharge the costs, expenses, debts, obligations, and liabilities, when all the property and assets have been applied so far as they will go to their payment according to the priorities set forth in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.753">302A.753</a>, the court shall enter a decree dissolving the corporation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.763.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Effective date.</span></h2>
          <p>When the decree dissolving the corporation has been entered, the corporation is dissolved.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.302A.763.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/270/#laws.0.114.0">1981 c 270 s 114</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.302A.765">
        <h1 class="shn">302A.765 FILING DECREE.</h1>
        <p>After the court enters a decree dissolving a corporation, the court administrator shall cause a certified copy of the decree to be filed with the secretary of state. The secretary of state shall not charge a fee for filing the decree.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.302A.765.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/270/#laws.0.115.0">1981 c 270 s 115</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/1/3/#laws.1.82.0">1Sp1986 c 3 art 1 s 82</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.302A.771">
        <h1 class="shn">302A.771 DEPOSIT WITH COMMISSIONER OF MANAGEMENT AND BUDGET OF AMOUNT DUE CERTAIN SHAREHOLDERS.</h1>
        <p>Upon dissolution of a corporation, the portion of the assets distributable to a shareholder who is unknown or cannot be found, or who is under disability, if there is no person legally competent to receive the distributive portion, shall be reduced to money and deposited with the commissioner of management and budget. The amount deposited is appropriated to the commissioner of management and budget and shall be paid over to the shareholder or a legal representative, upon proof satisfactory to the commissioner of management and budget of a right to payment.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.302A.771.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/270/#laws.0.116.0">1981 c 270 s 116</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/112/#laws.2.50.0">2003 c 112 art 2 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.302A.781">
        <h1 class="shn">302A.781 CLAIMS BARRED; EXCEPTIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.781.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Claims barred.</span></h2>
          <p>Except as provided in this section, a creditor or claimant whose claims are barred under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.727">302A.727</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.7291">302A.7291</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.759">302A.759</a> includes a person who is or becomes a creditor or claimant at any time before, during, or following the conclusion of dissolution proceedings, and all those claiming through or under the creditor or claimant.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.781.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Claims reopened.</span></h2>
          <p>At any time within one year after articles of dissolution have been filed with the secretary of state pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.727">302A.727</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.7291#stat.302A.7291.1">302A.7291, subdivision 1</a>, clause (2), or a decree of dissolution has been entered, a creditor or claimant who shows good cause for not having previously filed the claim may apply to a court in this state to allow a claim:</p>
          <p>(a) against the corporation to the extent of undistributed assets; or</p>
          <p>(b) if the undistributed assets are not sufficient to satisfy the claim, against a shareholder, whose liability shall be limited to a portion of the claim that is equal to the portion of the distributions to shareholders in liquidation or dissolution received by the shareholder, but in no event may a shareholder's liability exceed the amount which that shareholder actually received in the dissolution.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.781.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Obligations incurred during dissolution proceedings.</span></h2>
          <p>All known contractual debts, obligations, and liabilities incurred in the course of winding up the corporation's affairs shall be paid or provided for by the corporation before the distribution of assets to a shareholder. A person to whom this kind of debt, obligation, or liability is owed but not paid may pursue any remedy before the expiration of the applicable statute of limitations against the officers and directors of the corporation who are responsible for, but who fail to cause the corporation to pay or make provision for payment of the debts, obligations, and liabilities or against shareholders to the extent permitted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.559">302A.559</a>. This subdivision does not apply to dissolution under the supervision or order of a court.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.781.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Statutory homeowner warranty claims preserved.</span></h2>
          <p>The statutory warranties provided under section 327A.02, and any contribution or indemnity claim arising from the breach of those warranties, are not affected by the dissolution under this chapter of a vendor or home improvement contractor.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.781.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Other claims preserved.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">In addition to the claims in subdivision 4, all other statutory and common law rights of persons who may bring claims of injury to a person, including death, are not affected by dissolution under this chapter.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.302A.781.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/270/#laws.0.117.0">1981 c 270 s 117</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/497/#laws.0.67.0">1982 c 497 s 67</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/104/#laws.0.45.0">1987 c 104 s 45</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/49/#laws.0.27.0">1991 c 49 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/202/#laws.0.3.0">2006 c 202 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/54/#laws.5.6.0">2007 c 54 art 5 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/343/#laws.0.1.0">2010 c 343 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.302A.783">
        <h1 class="shn">302A.783 RIGHT TO SUE OR DEFEND AFTER DISSOLUTION.</h1>
        <p>After a corporation has been dissolved, any of its former officers, directors, or shareholders may assert or defend, in the name of the corporation, any claim by or against the corporation.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.302A.783.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/270/#laws.0.118.0">1981 c 270 s 118</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.302A.791">
        <h1 class="shn">302A.791 OMITTED ASSETS.</h1>
        <p>Title to assets remaining after payment of all debts, obligations, or liabilities and after distributions to shareholders may be transferred by a court in this state.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.302A.791.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/270/#laws.0.119.0">1981 c 270 s 119</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.302A.801"><b>302A.801</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/106/#laws.0.27.0">2011 c 106 s 27</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.302A.805"><b>302A.805</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/106/#laws.0.27.0">2011 c 106 s 27</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">CORPORATE REGISTRATION</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.302A.821">
        <h1 class="shn">302A.821 MINNESOTA CORPORATE RENEWAL.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.821.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>MS 1998 [Renumbered subd 2]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.821.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Annual renewal.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The secretary of state may send annually to each corporation, using the information provided by the corporation pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/5.002">5.002</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/5.34">5.34</a> or the articles of incorporation, a notice announcing the need to file the annual renewal and informing the corporation that the annual renewal may be filed online and that paper filings may also be made, and informing the corporation that failing to file the annual renewal will result in an administrative dissolution of the corporation.</p>
          <p>(b) Each calendar year beginning in the calendar year following the calendar year in which a corporation incorporates, the corporation must file with the secretary of state by December 31 of each calendar year a renewal containing the information listed in subdivision 2.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.821.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>MS 1998 [Renumbered subd 3]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.821.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Information required; manner of filing.</span></h2>
          <p>The filing must be made pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/5.34">5.34</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.821.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>MS 1998 [Repealed by amendment, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/395/#laws.0.5.0">2000 c 395 s 5</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.821.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed by amendment, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.73.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 73</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.821.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>MS 1998 [Repealed by amendment, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/395/#laws.0.5.0">2000 c 395 s 5</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.821.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Penalty; reinstatement.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) A corporation that has failed to file a renewal complying with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/5.34">5.34 </a>must be dissolved by the secretary of state as described in paragraph (b).</p>
          <p class="in">(b) If the corporation has not filed the renewal during any calendar year, the secretary of state must issue a certificate of administrative dissolution and the certificate must be filed in the Office of the Secretary of State. The secretary of state must make available in an electronic format the names of the dissolved corporations. A corporation dissolved in this manner is not entitled to the benefits of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.781">302A.781</a>. The liability, if any, of the shareholders of a corporation dissolved in this manner shall be determined and limited in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.557">302A.557</a>, except that the shareholders shall have no liability to any director of the corporation under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/302A.559#stat.302A.559.2">302A.559, subdivision 2</a>.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) After administrative dissolution, filing a renewal complying with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/5.34">5.34 </a>and the $25 fee with the secretary of state:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) returns the corporation to good standing as of the date of the dissolution;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) validates contracts or other acts within the authority of the articles, and the corporation is liable for those contracts or acts; and</p>
          <p class="in">(3) restores to the corporation all assets and rights of the corporation to the extent they were held by the corporation before the dissolution occurred, except to the extent that assets or rights were affected by acts occurring after the dissolution or sold or otherwise distributed after that time.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.821.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered subd 4]</p>

      <div class="history" id="stat.302A.821.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>

      <h1 class="fgh">ACTIONS AGAINST CORPORATIONS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.302A.901">
        <h1 class="shn">302A.901 SERVICE OF PROCESS ON CORPORATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.901.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Who may be served.</span></h2>
          <p>A process, notice, or demand required or permitted by law to be served upon a corporation may be served either upon the registered agent, if any, of the corporation named in the articles, or upon an officer of the corporation, or upon the secretary of state as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/5.25">5.25</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.901.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/128/#laws.1.20.0">1995 c 128 art 1 s 20</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.901.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/128/#laws.1.20.0">1995 c 128 art 1 s 20</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.901.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/128/#laws.1.20.0">1995 c 128 art 1 s 20</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.901.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/128/#laws.1.20.0">1995 c 128 art 1 s 20</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.302A.901.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/270/#laws.0.123.0">1981 c 270 s 123</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/3/2/#laws.1.35.0">3Sp1981 c 2 art 1 s 35</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/17/#laws.0.57.0">1993 c 17 s 57</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/128/#laws.1.6.0">1995 c 128 art 1 s 6</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.302A.917">
        <h1 class="shn">302A.917 STATE INTERESTED; PROCEEDINGS.</h1>
        <p>If it appears at any stage of a proceeding in a court in this state that the state is, or is likely to be, interested therein, or that it is a matter of general public interest, the court shall order that a copy of the complaint or petition be served upon the attorney general in the same manner prescribed for serving a summons in a civil action. The attorney general shall intervene in a proceeding when the attorney general determines that the public interest requires it, whether or not the attorney general has been served.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.302A.917.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/270/#laws.0.124.0">1981 c 270 s 124</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">CORPORATION CREATED BY SPECIAL ACT; TRUSTEES AND DIRECTORS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.302A.92">
        <h1 class="shn">302A.92 SELECTION OF TRUSTEES OR DIRECTORS BY A CORPORATION CREATED BY SPECIAL ACT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.92.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Resolution changing the method of selection.</span></h2>
          <p>A corporation created by a special act of the legislature of the Territory or the State of Minnesota which prescribes a method of selection of the trustees or directors of the corporation may change the method as to trustees or directors other than those automatically made trustees or directors by the special act. The method change must be made by adoption of a resolution by the body or persons empowered by the special act to select the trustees or directors. The corporation may provide in the resolution that those selected must hold office until their successors are selected and have qualified; that a vacancy in the office of trustee or director must be filled by the remaining trustees or directors; and that the appointee must hold office until the next annual meeting of the corporation, at which time a trustee or director will be elected in the manner provided to serve for the remainder of the unexpired term.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.92.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Filing of resolution.</span></h2>
          <p>A certified copy of the resolution referred to in subdivision 1 must be filed in the Office of the Secretary of State. The resolution takes effect when filed.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.92.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Effect of filing resolution.</span></h2>
          <p>After the resolution takes effect, the board of trustees or directors of the corporation are self-perpetuating. All vacancies are to be filled as provided in subdivision 1.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.302A.92.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Application of section.</span></h2>
          <p>This section does not apply to the Board of Regents of the University of Minnesota.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.302A.92.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1951/0/656/#laws.0.1.0">1951 c 656 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1955/0/520/#laws.0.1.0">1955 c 520 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/628/#laws.5.1.0">1984 c 628 art 5 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/69/#laws.1.21.0">2005 c 69 art 1 s 21</a></p>
      </div>
    </div>
